\vec{\xi}=\vec{\eta}
and
a_{\text{TRUE}}=a
.

According to Lemma
2
, the following holds for
a=a_{1},a_{2}
:

Let
Q>C
and
Q\leq(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{-1/1000}
, and suppose that

\begin{split}&\max_{\nu}(|\bar{q}_{\nu}|+|\bar{u}_{\nu}|)\leq Q,\\
&\Big|\sum_{0\leq\nu<N}(\bar{q}_{\nu+1}-\bar{q}_{\nu})^{2}-T\Big|\leq Q^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4},\end{split}

(6.1)

where
\bar{u}_{\nu}
denotes the value of
u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})
assuming
q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})=\bar{q}_{\mu}
for
\mu\leq\nu
. (Recall that the
\bar{u}_{\nu}
don’t depend on
a
.) Then

\begin{split}\Phi(\bar{q}_{1},\dots,\bar{q}_{N},a&)\\
=\prod_{0\leq\nu<N}\Big\{&\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\Delta t_{\nu}}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2\Delta t_{\nu}}[(\bar{q}_{\nu+1}-\bar{q}_{\nu})-(a\bar{q}_{\nu}+\bar{u}_{\nu})\Delta t_{\nu}]^{2}\Big)\Big\}\\
&\cdot(1+\text{ERR}(\bar{q}_{1},\dots,\bar{q}_{N},a)\end{split}

(6.2)

with

|\text{ERR}(\bar{q}_{1},\dots,\bar{q}_{N},a)|\leq CQ^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4}

(6.3)

and
\bar{q}_{0}\equiv q_{0}
.

Applying (
6.2
), (
6.3
) with
a=a_{1}
and with
a=a_{2}
, we see that if (
6.1
) holds, then

\begin{split}\Phi(\bar{q}_{1},\dots,\bar{q}_{N},a_{2})=&\Phi(\bar{q}_{1},\dots,\bar{q}_{N},a_{1})\\
&\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}[a_{2}^{2}-a_{1}^{2}]\bar{\zeta}_{2}^{\sigma}(T)+[a_{2}-a_{1}]\bar{\zeta}_{1}^{\sigma}(T)\Big)\\
&\cdot(1+\text{ERR}(\bar{q}_{1},\dots,\bar{q}_{N},a_{1},a_{2}))\end{split}

(6.4)

with

\bar{\zeta}_{1}^{\sigma}(T):=\sum_{0\leq\nu<N}\bar{q}_{\nu}([\bar{q}_{\nu+1}-\bar{q}_{\nu}]-\bar{u}_{\nu}\Delta t_{\nu}),\qquad\bar{\zeta}_{2}^{\sigma}(T):=\sum_{0\leq\nu<N}\bar{q}_{\nu}^{2}\Delta t_{\nu},

(6.5)

and

|\text{ERR}(\bar{q}_{1},\dots,\bar{q}_{N},a_{1},a_{2})|\leq CQ^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4}.

(6.6)

If (
6.1
) holds, then

|\bar{\zeta}_{1}^{\sigma}(T)|,|\bar{\zeta}_{2}^{\sigma}(T)|\leq CQ^{2}

(6.7)

(see (
2.17
)).

Letting
\mathcal{E}
denote the event that
(q^{\sigma}(t_{1}),\dots,q^{\sigma}(t_{N}))
satisfies (
6.1
), we conclude from (
6.4
), (
6.6
), (
6.7
) that

\begin{split}\text{E}_{a_{2},\vec{\eta}}[f(q^{\sigma}(t_{1}),&\dots,q^{\sigma}(t_{N}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}}]\\
\leq&\text{E}_{a_{1},\vec{\eta}}[f(q^{\sigma}(t_{1}),\dots,q^{\sigma}(t_{N}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\mathcal{E}}]\cdot\exp(CQ^{2}|a_{2}-a_{1}|)\\
&\cdot(1+\text{ERR}_{f}(a_{1},a_{2}))\end{split}

(6.8)

with

|\text{ERR}_{f}(a_{1},a_{2})|\leq CQ^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4}.

(6.9)

On the other hand, Lemma
1
shows that the complement of
\mathcal{E}
, denoted
\prescript{c}{}{\mathcal{E}}
, satisfies

\text{Prob}_{a_{2},\vec{\eta}}[\prescript{c}{}{\mathcal{E}}]\leq\exp(-cQ^{2}),

and therefore, by Cauchy-Schwarz, we have

\begin{split}\text{E}_{a_{2},\vec{\eta}}[f(q^{\sigma}(t_{1}),\dots,&q^{\sigma}(t_{N}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\prescript{c}{}{\mathcal{E}}}]\\
&\leq\exp(-c^{\prime}Q^{2})\Big(\text{E}_{a_{2},\vec{\eta}}[f^{2}(q^{\sigma}(t_{1}),\dots,q^{\sigma}(t_{N}))]\Big)^{1/2}.\end{split}

(6.10)

Combining (
6.9
) and (
6.10
), we obtain the following result.

Lemma 5

(Lemma on Change of Assumption)
.

Suppose
Q\geq C
for a large enough constant
C
.

Let
a_{1},a_{2}\in[-a_{\emph{MAX}},+a_{\emph{MAX}}]
, let
\vec{\eta}\in\{0,1\}^{\mathbb{N}}
, and let
f(\bar{q}_{1},\dots,\bar{q}_{N})
be a nonnegative function on
\mathbb{R}^{\mathbb{N}}
. Let
\sigma
be a tame strategy. Assume that
Q\leq(\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}})^{-1/1000}
. Then

\displaystyle\emph{E}_{a_{2},\vec{\eta}}[

\displaystyle f(q^{\sigma}(t_{1}),\dots,q^{\sigma}(t_{N}))]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp(CQ^{2}|a_{2}-a_{1}|)(1+CQ^{2}(\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}})^{1/4})\emph{E}_{a_{1},\vec{\eta}}[f(q^{\sigma}(t_{1}),\dots,q^{\sigma}(t_{N}))]

\displaystyle+\exp(-cQ^{2})\Big(\emph{E}_{a_{2},\vec{\eta}}[f^{2}(q^{\sigma}(t_{1}),\dots,q^{\sigma}(t_{N}))]\Big)^{1/2}.

7
Disasters Due to Undercontrol

Our tame strategies
\sigma
are defined to guarantee that

|u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\leq C[|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|+1],

(7.1)

which is reasonable, given our assumption that
a_{\text{TRUE}}\in[-a_{\text{MAX}},a_{\text{MAX}}]
. However, if
a_{\text{TRUE}}\gg a_{\text{MAX}}
, then we expect (
7.1
) to undercontrol, leading to exponentially large expected cost.

The following lemma confirms that intuition.

Lemma 6

(Lemma on Undercontrol)
.

Let
\sigma
be a deterministic strategy satisfying (
7.1
), and suppose
a_{\emph{TRUE}}=a
, where
a
exceeds a large enough constant
C_{*}
. Write
\emph{E}_{a}[\dots]
for the corresponding expectation. Assume that
\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}}
is less than a small enough positive number determined by
a
and
T
. Then

\emph{E}_{a}\Big[\sum_{0\leq\nu<N}\{q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}\}\Delta t_{\nu}\Big]\geq cT^{2}\exp(caT).

Proof.

We write
q_{\nu}
for
q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})
,
\Delta q_{\nu}
for
q_{\nu+1}-q_{\nu}
,
u_{\nu}
for
u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})
, and
\Delta t_{\nu}
for
t_{\nu+1}-t_{\nu}
. We let
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}
denote the sigma algebra of events determined by
q_{1},\dots,q_{\nu}
. Thus,
q_{\nu}
and
u_{\nu}
are deterministic once we condition on
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}
.

Recall that

\Delta q_{\nu}=(aq_{\nu}+u_{\nu})(\Delta t_{\nu}^{*})+\Delta W_{\nu}

(7.2)

where
\Delta W_{\nu}
is normal with mean 0 and variance

\Delta\tilde{t}_{\nu}=\frac{\exp(2a\Delta t_{\nu})-1}{2a};

moreover
\Delta W_{\nu}
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}
. Here,

\Delta t_{\nu}^{*}=\frac{\exp(a\Delta t_{\nu})-1}{a}.

Since
\Delta t_{\nu}<\Delta t_{\text{MAX}}
is less than a small enough positive number determined by
a
and
T
, we have

|\Delta\tilde{t}_{\nu}-\Delta t_{\nu}|,|\Delta t_{\nu}^{*}-\Delta t_{\nu}|<10^{-3}\Delta t_{\nu}.

(7.3)

From (
7.2
) we have

\displaystyle q_{\nu+1}^{2}=

\displaystyle q_{\nu}^{2}+2q_{\nu}[(aq_{\nu}+u_{\nu})(\Delta t_{\nu}^{*})+\Delta W_{\nu}]+(aq_{\nu}+u_{\nu})^{2}(\Delta t_{\nu}^{*})^{2}

\displaystyle+2(aq_{\nu}+u_{\nu})(\Delta t_{\nu}^{*})\Delta W_{\nu}+(\Delta W_{\nu})^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle q_{\nu}^{2}(1+2a\Delta t_{\nu}^{*})+2q_{\nu}u_{\nu}(\Delta t_{\nu}^{*})+[2q_{\nu}+2(aq_{\nu}+u_{\nu})(\Delta t_{\nu}^{*})]\Delta W_{\nu}

\displaystyle+(\Delta W_{\nu})^{2}.

Consequently,

\text{E}_{a}[q_{\nu+1}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}]\geq q_{\nu}^{2}(1+2a\Delta t_{\nu}^{*})+2q_{\nu}u_{\nu}(\Delta t_{\nu}^{*})+(\Delta\tilde{t}_{\nu}).

(7.4)

For
\delta>0
to be picked in a moment, we have

|2q_{\nu}u_{\nu}|\leq\delta^{-2}q_{\nu}^{2}+\delta^{2}u_{\nu}^{2}\leq C\delta^{-2}q_{\nu}^{2}+C\delta^{2},

thanks to (
7.1
).

Putting this inequality into (
7.4
), we find that

\text{E}_{a}[q_{\nu+1}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}]\geq q_{\nu}^{2}(1+[2a-C\delta^{-2}]\Delta t_{\nu}^{*})+(\Delta\tilde{t}_{\nu}-C\delta^{2}\Delta t_{\nu}^{*}).

(7.5)

We take
\delta
to be a small enough constant
c
such that

\Delta\tilde{t}_{\nu}-C\delta^{2}\Delta t_{\nu}^{*}\geq\frac{1}{2}\Delta t_{\nu};

see (
7.3
). Since
a
exceeds a large enough constant
C
, we then have

[2a-C\delta^{-2}]>a,

so from (
7.5
) we obtain

\text{E}_{a}[q_{\nu+1}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}]\geq q_{\nu}^{2}(1+a\Delta t_{\nu}^{*})+\frac{1}{2}\Delta t_{\nu}.

Again applying (
7.3
), and recalling that
\Delta t_{\nu}<\Delta t_{\text{MAX}}
is less than a small enough positive nuumber determined by
a
and
T
, we conclude that

\text{E}_{a}[q_{\nu+1}^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}]\geq\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}a\Delta t_{\nu}\Big)q_{\nu}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\Delta t_{\nu},

and therefore

\text{E}_{a}[q_{\nu+1}^{2}]\geq\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}a\Delta t_{\nu}\Big)\text{E}_{a}[q_{\nu}^{2}]+\frac{1}{2}\Delta t_{\nu}.

(7.6)

Since (
7.6
) implies that

\text{E}_{a}[q_{\nu+1}^{2}]\geq\text{E}_{a}[q_{\nu}^{2}]+\frac{1}{2}\Delta t_{\nu}\;\text{for each}\;\nu,

we conclude that

\text{E}_{a}[q_{\nu}^{2}]\geq\frac{1}{2}t_{\nu}\;\text{for each}\;\nu.

We pick
\nu_{0}
so that

\frac{1}{2}T<t_{\nu_{0}}<\frac{2}{3}T.

(Our smallness assumption on
\Delta t_{\text{MAX}}
implies that such a
\nu_{0}
exists.) Then

\text{E}_{a}[q_{\nu_{0}}^{2}]\geq\frac{1}{2}t_{\nu_{0}}>\frac{1}{4}T.

Returning to (
7.6
), we have

\text{E}_{a}[q_{\nu+1}^{2}]\geq\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}a\Delta t_{\nu}\Big)\text{E}_{a}[q_{\nu}^{2}]\;\text{for each}\;\nu,

hence for
\nu\geq\nu_{0}
we have

\text{E}_{a}[q_{\nu}^{2}]\geq\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}a[t_{\nu}-t_{\nu_{0}}]\Big)\text{E}[q_{\nu_{0}}^{2}]\geq\frac{1}{4}T\exp\Big(\frac{1}{2}a[t_{\nu}-t_{\nu_{0}}]\Big).

In particular,

\text{E}_{a}[q_{\nu}^{2}]\geq\frac{1}{4}T\exp(caT)\;\text{for}\;t_{\nu}\in\Big[\frac{3}{4}T,T\Big].

Consequently,

\displaystyle\text{E}_{a}\Big[\sum_{0\leq\nu<N}\{q_{\nu}^{2}+u_{\nu}^{2}\}\Delta t_{\nu}\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\text{E}_{a}\Big[\sum_{t_{\nu}\in[\frac{3}{4}T,T]}q_{\nu}^{2}\Delta t_{\nu}\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{4}T\exp(caT)\cdot\sum_{t_{\nu}\in[\frac{3}{4}T,T]}\Delta t_{\nu}

\displaystyle\geq cT^{2}\exp(caT),

since each
\Delta t_{\nu}<\Delta t_{\text{MAX}}
is less than a small enough positive number determined by
a
and
T
. The proof of the lemma is complete.
∎

8
Costing by Integrals

Let
\sigma
be a deterministic tame strategy. We condition on

a_{\text{TRUE}}=a\in[-a_{\text{MAX}},+a_{\text{MAX}}],

and write
\text{Prob}_{a}[\cdot]
and
E_{a}[\cdot]
to denote the corresponding probability and expectation.

We want to compare

\textsc{Cost}(\sigma)=\int_{0}^{T}\{(q^{\sigma}(t))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t))^{2}\}\ dt

with

\textsc{Cost}_{D}(\sigma)=\sum_{0\leq\nu<N}\{(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}\}\ \Delta t_{\nu}

(“
D
” for “discrete”).

Lemma 7

(Lemma on Costing by Integrals)
.

For any
m\geq 1
, we have

\emph{E}_{a}[|\textsc{Cost}(\sigma)-\textsc{Cost}_{D}(\sigma)|^{m}]\leq C_{m}(\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}})^{m/2}.

Proof.

Recall that
u^{\sigma}(t)=u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})
for
t\in[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1}]
. Hence,

\displaystyle\textsc{Cost}(\sigma)-

\displaystyle\textsc{Cost}_{D}(\sigma)=\sum_{0\leq\nu<N}\int_{t_{\nu}}^{t_{\nu+1}}\{(q^{\sigma}(t))^{2}-(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}\}\ dt

\displaystyle=\sum_{0\leq\nu<N}\int_{t_{\nu}}^{t_{\nu+1}}\{[q^{\sigma}(t)-q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})]^{2}+2q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})[q^{\sigma}(t)-q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})]\}\ dt.

Setting

\textsc{Osc}(\nu)=\max_{t\in[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1}]}|q^{\sigma}(t)-q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|,

we therefore have

|\textsc{Cost}(\sigma)-\textsc{Cost}_{D}(\sigma)|\leq\sum_{0\leq\nu<N}\{(\textsc{Osc}(\nu))^{2}+2|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|(\textsc{Osc}(\nu))\}\Delta t_{\nu},

so that by Hölder’s inequality,

|\textsc{Cost}(\sigma)-\textsc{Cost}_{D}(\sigma)|^{m}\\
\leq C_{m}\sum_{0\leq\nu<N}\{(\textsc{Osc}(\nu))^{2m}+|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|^{m}(\textsc{Osc}(\nu))^{m}\}\Delta t_{\nu}.

Consequently,

\begin{split}\text{E}_{a}[|\textsc{Cost}(\sigma)-&\textsc{Cost}_{D}(\sigma)|^{m}]\\
\leq&C_{m}\sum_{0\leq\nu<N}\text{E}_{a}[(\textsc{Osc}(\nu)^{2m}](\Delta t_{\nu})\\
&+C_{m}\sum_{0\leq\nu<N}(\text{E}_{a}[|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|^{2m}])^{1/2}(\text{E}_{a}[(\textsc{Osc}(\nu))^{2m}])^{1/2}\Delta t_{\nu}.\end{split}

(8.1)

We now estimate the right-hand side of (
8.1
).

Fix a large enough constant
C_{*}
, and let
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}
denote the sigma algebra of events determined by
q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})
for
\mu=1,\dots,\nu
.

Lemma
1
shows that

\text{E}_{a}[|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|^{2m}]\leq C_{m}

(8.2)

and that, given
Q_{2}\geq Q_{1}\geq C_{*}
, we have

\text{Prob}_{a}[\textsc{Osc}(\nu)>Q_{2}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{1/2}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}]\leq C\exp(-cQ_{2}^{2})\;\text{if}\;|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\leq Q_{1}.

(8.3)

From (
8.3
) we see that

\text{E}_{a}[(\textsc{Osc}(\nu))^{2m}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}]\leq C_{m}Q_{1}^{2m}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{m}\;\text{if}\;|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\leq Q_{1}.

In particular,

\text{E}_{a}[(\textsc{Osc}(\nu))^{2m}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\leq C_{*}}]\leq C_{m}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{m}

(8.4)

and, for
k\geq 0
,

\text{E}_{a}[(\textsc{Osc}(\nu))^{2m}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\in[C_{*}2^{k},C_{*}2^{(k+1)})}]\\
\leq C_{m}\cdot(C_{*}2^{(k+1)})^{2m}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{m}\cdot\text{Prob}_{a}[|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|>C_{*}2^{k}].

(8.5)

Another application of Lemma
1
gives

\text{Prob}_{a}[|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|>C_{*}2^{k}]\leq C\exp(-c2^{2k}),

so that (
8.5
) implies that

\text{E}_{a}[(\textsc{Osc}(\nu))^{2m}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\in[C_{*}2^{k},C_{*}2^{(k+1)})}]\leq C_{m}\cdot(2^{2mk})(\Delta t_{\nu})^{m}\cdot\exp(-c2^{2k}).

Summing over
k\geq 0
, and combining the result with (
8.4
), we learn that

\text{E}_{a}[(\textsc{Osc}(\nu))^{2m}]\leq C_{m}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{m}.

(8.6)

From (
8.1
), (
8.2
), and (
8.6
) we conclude that

\text{E}_{a}[|\textsc{Cost}(\sigma)-\textsc{Cost}_{D}(\sigma)|^{m}]\leq C_{m}\sum_{0\leq\nu<N}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{m/2+1}\leq C_{m}^{\prime}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{m/2},

which is the conclusion of the lemma.
∎

9
Continuous vs Discrete

Let
\sigma
be a tame strategy, possibly depending on coin flips
\vec{\xi}
. In this section we compare the following random functions of time.

\displaystyle q^{\sigma}_{C}(t)=q^{\sigma}(t)\;\text{for}\;t\in[0,T]

\displaystyle q^{\sigma}_{D}(t)=q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})\;\text{for}\;t\in[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1}),\;\text{each}\;\nu<N.

\displaystyle\zeta_{1,C}^{\sigma}(t)=\frac{1}{2}(q^{\sigma}(t))^{2}-\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}t-\int_{0}^{t}u^{\sigma}(s)q^{\sigma}(s)\ ds\;\text{for}\;t\in[0,T].

\displaystyle\zeta_{1,D}^{\sigma}(t)=\frac{1}{2}(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}-\frac{1}{2}q_{0}^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\sum_{0\leq\mu<\nu}(\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}-\sum_{0\leq\mu<\nu}u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})\ \Delta t_{\mu}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\text{for}\;t\in[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1}),\;\text{each}\;\nu<N.

\displaystyle\zeta_{2,C}^{\sigma}(t)=\int_{0}^{t}(q^{\sigma}(s))^{2}\ ds\;\text{for}\;t\in[0,T].

\displaystyle\zeta_{2,D}^{\sigma}(t)=\sum_{0\leq\mu<\nu}(q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}\;\text{for}\;t\in[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1}),\;\text{each}\;\nu<N.

We recall that
u^{\sigma}(t)
is constant on
[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1})
for each
\nu
, so there is no need to introduce analogous quantities for
u^{\sigma}
.

We establish the following result.

Lemma 8

(Lemma on Continuous Variants)
.

Let
Q
be greater than a large enough constant
C
. Then there exists an event
\textsc{bad}(\sigma,Q)
with the following properties.

•

For each
a\in[-a_{\emph{MAX}},+a_{\emph{MAX}}]
and
\vec{\eta}\in\{0,1\}^{\mathbb{N}}
, we have

\emph{Prob}_{a,\vec{\eta}}[\textsc{bad}(\sigma,Q)]\leq C\exp(-cQ^{2}).

•

If
\textsc{bad}(\sigma,Q)
does not occur, then

\displaystyle\max_{t\in[0,T)}|q^{\sigma}_{C}(t)-q^{\sigma}_{D}(t)|\leq CQ(\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}})^{1/4}

\displaystyle\max_{t\in[0,T)}|\zeta_{1,C}^{\sigma}(t)-\zeta_{1,D}^{\sigma}(t)|\leq CQ^{2}(\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}})^{1/4},

\displaystyle\max_{t\in[0,T)}|\zeta_{2,C}^{\sigma}(t)-\zeta_{2,D}^{\sigma}(t)|\leq CQ^{2}(\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}})^{1/4}.

Proof.

From Lemma
1
, the following have probability at most
C\exp(-cQ^{2})
when conditioned on
a_{\text{TRUE}}=a
,
\vec{\xi}=\vec{\eta}
(any
a\in[-a_{\text{MAX}},a_{\text{MAX}}]
,
\vec{\eta}\in\{0,1\}^{\mathbb{N}}):

\displaystyle\max_{\nu}\Big|\sum_{0\leq\mu<\nu}(\Delta q^{\sigma}_{\mu})^{2}-t_{\nu}\Big|>Q^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/2}

(9.1)

\displaystyle\max_{\mu}|q^{\sigma}_{\mu}|>Q

(9.2)

\displaystyle\max_{\mu}|u^{\sigma}_{\mu}|>Q.

(9.3)

Moreover, with

\textsc{Osc}(\nu):=\max_{t\in[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1}]}|q^{\sigma}(t)-q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|

and
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}
defined as the sigma algebra of events determined by the
q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})
for
\mu\leq\nu
, Lemma
1
gives also the following, for each fixed
\nu
.

\text{Prob}_{a,\vec{\eta}}[\textsc{Osc}(\nu)>CQ(\Delta t_{\nu})^{1/2}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}]\leq C\exp(-cQ^{2})\;\text{if}\;|q_{\nu}|\leq Q.

This implies that

\text{Prob}_{a,\vec{\eta}}[\textsc{Osc}(\nu)>CQ(\Delta t_{\nu})^{1/2}\;\text{and}\;|q_{\nu}|\leq Q]\leq C\exp(-cQ^{2}).

Since also

\text{Prob}_{a,\vec{\eta}}[|q_{\nu}|>Q]\leq C\exp(-cQ^{2}),

it follows that

\text{Prob}_{a,\vec{\eta}}[\textsc{Osc}(\nu)>CQ(\Delta t_{\nu})^{1/2}]\leq C\exp(-cQ^{2})

for each fixed
\nu
, and for all
Q\geq C
.

Taking
Q(\Delta t_{\nu})^{-1/4}
in place of
Q
here, we find that

\text{Prob}_{a,\vec{\eta}}[\textsc{Osc}(\nu)>CQ(\Delta t_{\nu})^{1/4}]\\
\leq C\exp(-cQ^{2}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{-1/2})\leq C^{\prime}\exp(-cQ^{2})(\Delta t_{\nu}).

Summing over
\nu
, we find that the event

\textsc{Osc}(\nu)>CQ(\Delta t_{\nu})^{1/4}\;\text{for some}\;\nu,

(9.4)

conditioned on
a_{\text{TRUE}}=a
and
\vec{\xi}=\vec{\eta}
, has probability at most
C\exp(-cQ^{2})
.

We now define
\textsc{bad}(Q,\sigma)
to be the event that at least one of the conditions (
9.1
)–(
9.4
) holds. Then, as claimed,

\text{Prob}_{a,\vec{\eta}}[\textsc{bad}(Q,\sigma)]\leq C\exp(-cQ^{2})

for any
a\in[-a_{\text{MAX}},+a_{\text{MAX}}]
and any
\vec{\eta}\in\{0,1\}^{\mathbb{N}}
.

We now suppose that
\textsc{bad}(Q,\sigma)
does not occur, and compare
q^{\sigma}_{C}(t)
with
q^{\sigma}_{D}(t)
,
\zeta_{1,C}^{\sigma}(t)
with
\zeta_{1,D}^{\sigma}(t)
, and
\zeta_{2,C}^{\sigma}(t)
with
\zeta_{2,D}^{\sigma}(t)
.

Since
\textsc{bad}(Q,\sigma)
does not occur, we have

\displaystyle\max_{\nu}|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|,\max_{\nu}|u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\leq Q,

(9.5)

\displaystyle\max_{\nu}\Big|\sum_{0\leq\mu<\nu}(\Delta q^{\sigma}_{\nu})^{2}-t_{\nu}\Big|\leq Q^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/2},

(9.6)

\displaystyle\max_{\nu}\textsc{Osc}(\nu)\leq CQ(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4}.

(9.7)

For any
\nu
, and any
t\in[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1})
, we have

|q^{\sigma}_{C}(t)-q^{\sigma}_{D}(t)|=|q^{\sigma}(t)-q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\leq\textsc{Osc}(\nu)\leq CQ(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4}.

Thus,

\max_{t\in[0,T]}|q^{\sigma}_{C}(t)-q^{\sigma}_{D}(t)|\leq CQ(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4},

as claimed. Next, for any
\nu
and any
t\in[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1})
, we have

\displaystyle\zeta_{1,C}^{\sigma}(t)-\zeta_{1,D}^{\sigma}(t)=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}[(q^{\sigma}(t))^{2}-(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]+\frac{1}{2}\Big[\sum_{0\leq\mu<\nu}(\Delta q^{\sigma}_{\mu})^{2}-t_{\nu}\Big]

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}(t-t_{\nu})-\sum_{0\leq\mu<\nu}\int_{t_{\mu}}^{t_{\mu+1}}\{u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})[q^{\sigma}(s)-q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})]\}\ ds

\displaystyle-\int_{t_{\nu}}^{t}u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})q^{\sigma}(s)\ ds

\displaystyle\equiv

\displaystyle\textsc{Term}\;1+\textsc{Term}\;2-\textsc{Term}\;3-\textsc{Term}\;4-\textsc{Term}\;5.

(Here, we have used the fact that
u^{\sigma}(s)=u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})
for
s\in[t_{\mu},t_{\mu+1})
.) We note that

\max_{t\in[0,T]}|q^{\sigma}(t)|\leq\max_{0\leq\nu<N}\{|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|+\textsc{Osc}(\nu)\}\leq CQ,

(9.8)

by (
9.5
) and (
9.7
). Hence, for
t\in[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1})
, we have

\displaystyle|\textsc{Term}\;1|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\tilde{t}\in[0,T]}|q^{\sigma}(\tilde{t})|\cdot|q^{\sigma}(t)-q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|

\displaystyle\leq CQ\textsc{Osc}(\nu)\leq CQ^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4}.

So

|\textsc{Term}\;1|\leq CQ^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4}\;\text{for all}\;t\in[0,T).

Next, (
9.6
) tells us that

|\textsc{Term}\;2|\leq Q^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/2}\;\text{for all}\;t\in[0,T).

Clearly

|\textsc{Term}\;3|\leq(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}}).

Furthermore,

\displaystyle|\textsc{Term}\;4|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{0\leq\mu<N}|u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})|\int_{t_{\mu}}^{t_{\mu+1}}|q^{\sigma}(s)-q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})|\ ds

\displaystyle\leq CQ\sum_{0\leq\mu<N}\textsc{Osc}(\mu)\Delta t_{\mu}\leq CQ\cdot\max_{\mu}\textsc{Osc}(\mu)\leq CQ^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4}

thanks to (
9.5
) and (
9.7
). Finally,

\displaystyle|\textsc{Term}\;5|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{\nu}|u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\cdot\max_{\tilde{t}\in[0,T]}|q^{\sigma}(\tilde{t})|\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})

\displaystyle\leq CQ^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}}),

by (
9.5
) and (
9.8
). Combining our estimates for
Terms
1–5, we find that

|\zeta_{1,C}^{\sigma}(t)-\zeta_{1,D}^{\sigma}(t)|\leq CQ^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4},

as claimed. We pass to
\zeta_{2,C}^{\sigma}
,
\zeta_{2,D}^{\sigma}
. For
t\in[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1})
, we have

\displaystyle|\zeta_{2,C}^{\sigma}(t)-\zeta_{2,D}^{\sigma}(t)|=

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{0\leq\mu<\nu}\int_{t_{\mu}}^{t_{\mu+1}}\{(q^{\sigma}(s))^{2}-(q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\}\ ds+\int_{t_{\nu}}^{t}(q^{\sigma}(s))^{2}\ ds\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{0\leq\mu<\nu}C\Big(\max_{\tilde{t}\in[0,T]}|q^{\sigma}(\tilde{t})|\Big)\int_{t_{\mu}}^{t_{\mu+1}}|q^{\sigma}(s)-q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})|\ ds

\displaystyle+\max_{\tilde{t}\in[0,T]}|q^{\sigma}(\tilde{t})|^{2}\cdot\Delta t_{\text{MAX}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle CQ\sum_{0\leq\mu<\nu}\textsc{Osc}(\mu)\Delta t_{\mu}+CQ^{2}\Delta t_{\text{MAX}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\prime}Q\max_{0\leq\mu<N}\textsc{Osc}(\mu)+CQ^{2}\Delta t_{\text{MAX}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\prime\prime}Q^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4},\;\text{thanks to \eqref{eq: cvd 7} and \eqref{eq: cvd 8}}.

The proof of the lemma is complete.
∎

10
Refining a Partition

Let
\sigma
be a tame strategy
\sigma=(\sigma_{t_{\nu}})_{0\leq\nu<N}
associated to a partition

0=t_{0}<t_{1}<\dots<t_{N}=T.

(10.1)

Suppose

0=\hat{t}_{0}<\hat{t}_{1}<\dots<\hat{t}_{\hat{N}}=T

(10.2)

is a refinement of the partition (
10.1
).

We would like to associate a tame strategy
\hat{\sigma}=(\hat{\sigma}_{\hat{t}_{\mu}})_{0\leq\mu<{\hat{N}}}
to the partition (
10.2
) in such a way that

\begin{split}&q^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)=q^{\sigma}(t)\;\text{for all}\;t\in[0,T],\;\text{and}\\
&u^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)=u^{\sigma}(t)\;\text{for all}\;t\in[0,T).\end{split}

(10.3)

That would tell us that refining the partition (
10.1
) allows additional tame strategies, but doesn’t rule out any tame strategies
\sigma
.

Unfortunately, no such
\hat{\sigma}
exists. The problem is that, in order to be a tame strategy,
\hat{\sigma}
must satisfy

|u^{\hat{\sigma}}(\hat{t}_{\mu})|\leq C_{\text{TAME}}[|q^{\hat{\sigma}}(\hat{t}_{\mu})|+1]\;\text{with probability}\;1.

(10.4)

It may happen that

|u^{\sigma}(\hat{t}_{\mu})|=|u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\gg C_{\text{TAME}}\cdot[|q^{\sigma}(\hat{t}_{\mu})|+1]

for some
\hat{t}_{\mu}\in(t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1})
, in which case (
10.4
) contradicts (
10.3
). Accordingly, we modify (
10.3
), as follows.

For each
\mu
(
0\leq\mu<{\hat{N}}
), define
\bar{\nu}(\mu)
to be the index
\nu
for which
t_{\nu}\leq\hat{t}_{\mu}<t_{\nu+1}
.

Define a stopping time
\tau
by setting

\tau=\begin{cases}\text{Least }\hat{t}_{\mu}\;\text{s.t. }|u^{\sigma}(t_{\bar{\nu}(\mu)})|>2C_{\text{TAME}}[|q^{\sigma}(\hat{t}_{\mu})|+1],&\text{if such a }\hat{t}_{\mu}\;\text{exists},\\
T&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Then define random processes
\hat{q}(t)
,
\hat{u}(t)
(
t\in[0,T]
) by setting

\begin{cases}\hat{q}(t)=q^{\sigma}(t)\;\text{and}\;\hat{u}(t)=u^{\sigma}(t)&\text{for}\;0\leq t<\tau.\\
\hat{u}(t)=0&\text{for}\;\tau\leq t\leq T,\end{cases}

and on
[\tau,T]
defining
\hat{q}
by

\begin{cases}d\hat{q}(t)=\big(a_{\text{TRUE}}\cdot\hat{q}(t)\big)dt+dW(t)\\
\text{with initial condition}\;\hat{q}(\tau)=q^{\sigma}(\tau).\end{cases}

Note that

|\hat{u}(\hat{t}_{\mu})|\leq 2C_{\text{TAME}}[|\hat{q}(\hat{t}_{\mu})|+1]\;\text{for all}\;\hat{t}_{\mu}.

(10.5)

It is a tedious exercise (left to the reader) to exhibit a tame strategy
\hat{\sigma}=(\hat{\sigma}_{\hat{t}_{\mu}})_{0\leq\mu<{\hat{N}}}
associated to the partition (
10.2
), such that
\hat{q}(t)=q^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)
for all
t\in[0,T]
and
\hat{u}(t)=u^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)
for all
t\in[0,T)
.

Whereas our original tame strategy
\sigma
satisfies

|\sigma_{t_{\nu}}(q_{1},\dots,q_{\nu},\vec{\xi})|\leq C_{\text{TAME}}[|q_{\nu}|+1],

(10.6)

the strategy
\hat{\sigma}
satisfies instead

|\hat{\sigma}_{\hat{t}_{\mu}}(q_{1},\dots,q_{\mu},\vec{\xi})|\leq 2C_{\text{TAME}}[|q_{\mu}|+1];

(10.7)

compare with (
10.5
).

In place of (
10.3
), we will show that
q^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)
and
u^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)
are likely very close to
q^{\sigma}(t)
,
u^{\sigma}(t)
, respectively. To see this, we fix
a\in[-a_{\text{MAX}},a_{\text{MAX}}]
and
\vec{\eta}\in\{0,1\}^{\mathbb{N}}
, and condition on
a_{\text{TRUE}}=a
,
\vec{\xi}=\vec{\eta}
.

We define random variables

\textsc{Osc}(\nu)=\max_{t\in[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1}]}|q^{\sigma}(t)-q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|

(10.8)

and an event

\textsc{disaster}:\;\textsc{Osc}(\nu)\geq 1\;\text{for some}\;\nu.

(10.9)

In Section
8
, we proved that

\text{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}[(\textsc{Osc}(\nu))^{m}]\leq C_{m}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{m/2}\;\text{for all}\;m\geq 1.

(10.10)

Hence,

\text{Prob}_{a,\vec{\eta}}[\textsc{Osc}(\nu)\geq 1]\leq C_{\bar{m}}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{\bar{m}}\;\text{for all}\;\bar{m}\geq 1,

and consequently

\text{Prob}_{a,\vec{\eta}}[\textsc{disaster}]\leq\sum_{0\leq\nu<N}C_{\bar{m}}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{\bar{m}}\leq C_{\bar{m}-1}^{\prime}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{\bar{m}-1}

(10.11)

for any
\bar{m}\geq 1
.

Next, we prepare to estimate

\int_{0}^{T}\{|q^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)-q^{\sigma}(t)|^{m}+|u^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)-u^{\sigma}(t)|^{m}\}\ dt.

Let

q^{\sigma}_{0}(t)=q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})\;\text{for }t\in[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1}),\;0\leq\nu<N.

Then

\int_{t_{\nu}}^{t_{\nu+1}}|q^{\sigma}_{0}(t)-q^{\sigma}(t)|^{2m}\;dt\leq(\textsc{Osc}(\nu))^{2m}\Delta t_{\nu},

hence

\begin{split}\text{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}|q^{\sigma}_{0}(t)-q^{\sigma}(t)|^{2m}\ dt\Big]&\leq\sum_{\nu}\text{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}[(\textsc{Osc}(\nu))^{2m}]\Delta t_{\nu}\\
&\leq C_{m}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{m},\end{split}

(10.12)

thanks to (
10.10
). Similarly,

\text{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}|q^{\hat{\sigma}}_{0}(t)-q^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)|^{2m}\ dt\Big]\leq C_{m}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{m},

(10.13)

where
q^{\hat{\sigma}}_{0}(t)=q^{\hat{\sigma}}(\hat{t}_{\mu})
for
t\in[\hat{t}_{\mu},\hat{t}_{\mu+1})
,
0\leq\mu<{\hat{N}}
. We have also

\text{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}|q^{\sigma}_{0}(t)|^{2m}\ dt\Big]=\text{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}\Big[\sum_{0\leq\nu<N}|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|^{2m}\Delta t_{\nu}\Big]\leq C_{m},

(10.14)

thanks to Lemma
1
. Similarly,

\text{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}|q^{\hat{\sigma}}_{0}(t)|^{2m}\ dt\Big]\leq C_{m}.

(10.15)

From (
10.12
) and (
10.14
) we obtain

\text{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}|q^{\sigma}(t)|^{2m}\ dt\Big]\leq C_{m}\;\text{for}\;m\geq 1.

(10.16)

Similarly, from (
10.13
) and (
10.15
), we have

\text{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}|q^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)|^{2m}\ dt\Big]\leq C_{m}\;\text{for }m\geq 1.

(10.17)

Turning to
u^{\sigma}
and
u^{\hat{\sigma}}
, we recall that
u^{\sigma}(t)=u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})
for
t\in[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1})
,
0\leq\nu<N
; hence,

\text{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}|u^{\sigma}(t)|^{2m}\ dt\Big]=\text{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}\Big[\sum_{0\leq\nu<N}|u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|^{2m}\Delta t_{\nu}\Big]\leq C_{m}\;(m\geq 1),

(10.18)

by Lemma
1
. Similarly

\text{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}|u^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)|^{2m}\ dt\Big]\leq C_{m}\;(m\geq 1).

(10.19)

From (
10.16
)–(
10.18
) we see that

\text{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}\{|q^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)-q^{\sigma}(t)|+|u^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)-u^{\sigma}(t)|\}^{2m}\ dt\Big]\leq C_{m}\;(m\geq 1).

(10.20)

Moreover, unless
disaster
occurs, we have
\tau=T
, hence

q^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)=q^{\sigma}(t)\;\text{and}\;u^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)=u^{\sigma}(t)\;\text{for all}\;t\in[0,T].

(10.21)

Indeed, if
disaster
doesn’t occur, then for
0\leq\nu<N
and
t\in[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1})
we have

|q^{\sigma}(t)-q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\leq 1,

hence
[|q^{\sigma}(t)|+1]
and
[|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|+1]
differ by at most a factor of 2. Since
|u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\leq C_{\text{TAME}}[|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|+1]
, it follows that
|u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\leq 2C_{\text{TAME}}[|q^{\sigma}(t)|+1]
for
t\in[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1})
,
0\leq\nu<N
. In particular,

|u^{\sigma}(t_{\bar{\nu}(\mu)})|\leq 2C_{\text{TAME}}[|q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})|+1]\;\text{for all}\;\mu\;(0\leq\mu<{\hat{N}}).

(10.22)

Comparing (
10.22
) with the definition of
\tau
, we see that, as claimed,
\tau=T
unless
disaster
occurs.

Thus (
10.21
) holds unless
disaster
occurs.

From (
10.20
), (
10.21
), (
10.11
), we now have

\begin{split}\text{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}&\{|q^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)-q^{\sigma}(t)|+|u^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)-u^{\sigma}(t)|\}^{m}\ dt\Big]\\
=&\text{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}\{|q^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)-q^{\sigma}(t)|+|u^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)-u^{\sigma}(t)|\}^{m}\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{disaster}}\ dt\Big]\\
\leq&\Big(\text{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}\{|q^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)-q^{\sigma}(t)|+|u^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)-u^{\sigma}(t)|\}^{2m}\ dt\Big]\Big)^{1/2}\\
&\cdot(\text{Prob}_{a,\vec{\eta}}[\textsc{disaster}])^{1/2}\\
\leq&C_{m,\bar{m}}\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{(\bar{m}-1)/2}\;\text{for any}\;m,\bar{m}\geq 1.\end{split}

(10.23)

We record this result as a lemma.

Lemma 9

(Refinement Lemma)
.

Let
\sigma
be a tame strategy associated to a partition

0=t_{0}<t_{1}<\dots<t_{N}=T,

(A)

and let

0=\hat{t}_{0}<\hat{t}_{1}<\dots<\hat{t}_{\hat{N}}=T

(B)

be a refinement of the partition
(A)
.

Then there exists a tame strategy
\hat{\sigma}
associated to the partition
(B)
, such that

\emph{E}_{a,\vec{\eta}}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}\{|q^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)-q^{\sigma}(t)|+|u^{\hat{\sigma}}(t)-u^{\sigma}(t)|\}^{m}\ dt\Big]\leq C_{m,\bar{m}}(\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}})^{\bar{m}}

for all
m,\bar{m}\geq 1
and all
a\in[-a_{\emph{MAX}},+a_{\emph{MAX}}]
,
\vec{\eta}\in\{0,1\}^{\mathbb{N}}
.

The strategy
\hat{\sigma}
satisfies the same estimates as we assumed for
\sigma
(see
7.1
), except that
C_{\emph{TAME}}
is replaced by
2C_{\emph{TAME}}
.

Chapter 3
Bayesian Strategies Associated to Partitions

1
Setup

In this chapter, we take
a_{\text{TRUE}}
to be governed by a known prior probability distribution
d\text{Prior}(a)
, concentrated on an interval
[-a_{\text{MAX}},+a_{\text{MAX}}]
.

We fix a partition

0=t_{0}<t_{1}<\dots<t_{N}=T

(1.1)

of the time interval
[0,T]
.

We fix a deterministic strategy
\sigma
for the game starting at position
q_{0}
.

We assume that our strategy
\sigma
is tame, i.e.,

|u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\leq C_{\text{TAME}}^{\sigma}[|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|+1]

(1.2)

for a constant
C_{\text{TAME}}^{\sigma}
. We call
C_{\text{TAME}}^{\sigma}
the
tame constant
for
\sigma
.

We write

\displaystyle q_{\nu}^{\sigma}=q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),

\displaystyle\Delta q_{\nu}^{\sigma}=q_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}-q_{\nu}^{\sigma},

\displaystyle\zeta_{1,\nu}^{\sigma}=\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),

\displaystyle\Delta\zeta_{1,\nu}^{\sigma}=\zeta_{1,\nu+1}^{\sigma}-\zeta_{1,\nu}^{\sigma},

\displaystyle\zeta_{2,\nu}^{\sigma}=\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),

\displaystyle\Delta\zeta_{2,\nu}^{\sigma}=\zeta_{2,\nu+1}^{\sigma}-\zeta_{2,\nu}^{\sigma},

\displaystyle u^{\sigma}_{\nu}=u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}).

Until further notice,
c,C,C^{\prime},
etc. will denote constants determined by
C_{\text{TAME}}^{\sigma}
in (
1.2
) together with
a_{\text{MAX}}
and upper bounds for
T
and
|q_{0}|
. The symbols
c,C,C^{\prime},
etc. may denote different constants in different occurrences. We assume that

\Delta t_{\text{MAX}}\equiv\max_{0\leq\nu<N}(t_{\nu+1}-t_{\nu})<c\;\text{for a small enough constant }c.

(1.3)

We write
X=O(Y)
to indicate that
|X|\leq CY
. We write
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}
to denote the sigma algebra of events determined by
q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})
for
\mu=0,1,\dots,\nu
. Note that
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}
depends on
\sigma
, because
a_{\text{TRUE}}
isn’t deterministic.

We write
\text{Prob}[\dots]
to denote probability, and we write
\text{E}[\dots]
to denote expectation.

If we condition on
a_{\text{TRUE}}=a
, then we write
\text{Prob}_{a}[\dots]
and
\text{E}_{a}[\dots]
to denote the corresponding probability and expectation. Thus, for instance,
\text{E}_{a}[X|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]
dentoes the expected value of
X
conditioned on
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}
, given that
a_{\text{TRUE}}=a
.

For any event
\mathcal{E}
we have

\text{Prob}[\mathcal{E}]=\int_{-a_{\text{MAX}}}^{a_{\text{MAX}}}\text{Prob}_{a}[\mathcal{E}]\ d\text{Prior}(a)

(1.4)

and

\text{Prob}[\mathcal{E}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=\int_{-a_{\text{MAX}}}^{a_{\text{MAX}}}\text{Prob}_{a}[\mathcal{E}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\ d\text{Post}(a|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}),

(1.5)

where
d\text{Post}(a|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma})
is the posterior probability distribution for
a_{\text{TRUE}}
conditioned on
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}
.

Similarly, for any random variable
X
, we have

\text{E}[X]=\int_{-a_{\text{MAX}}}^{a_{\text{MAX}}}\text{E}_{a}[X]\ d\text{Prior}(a)

(1.6)

and

\text{E}[X|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=\int_{-a_{\text{MAX}}}^{a_{\text{MAX}}}\text{E}_{a}[X|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\ d\text{Post}(a|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}).

(1.7)

Thanks to (
1.4
)–(
1.7
), the following results are immediate from Lemma
1
.

Lemma 10
.

[Bayesian Lemma on Rare Events]
Suppose
Q>C
for a large enough
C
. Then the following hold with probability
>1-\exp(-cQ^{2})
:

•

|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|,|u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\leq Q
, for all
\nu
.

•

|\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|,|\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\leq Q^{2}
, for all
\nu
.

•

|\sum_{0\leq\mu<\nu}(q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu+1})-q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}-t_{\nu}|\leq Q^{2}(\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}})^{1/2}
for all
\nu
.

Moreover, suppose we fix
\nu
and condition on
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}
, the sigma algebra of events determined by
q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})
for
0\leq\mu\leq\nu
. Suppose
|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\leq Q
, where
C\leq Q\leq(\Delta t_{\nu})^{-1/1000}
(for large enough
C
).

Then for
p=1,2
, we have

\emph{E}[(|\Delta q^{\sigma}_{\nu}|+|\Delta\zeta_{1,\nu}^{\sigma}|+|\Delta\zeta_{2,\nu}^{\sigma}|)^{p}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{|\Delta q^{\sigma}_{\nu}|+|\Delta\zeta_{1,\nu}^{\sigma}|+|\Delta\zeta_{2,\nu}^{\sigma}|>(\Delta t_{\nu})^{2/5}}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
\leq C\cdot(\Delta t_{\nu})^{100};

also

\emph{Prob}\Big[\max_{t\in[t_{\nu},t_{\nu+1}]}|q^{\sigma}(t)-q^{\sigma}_{\nu}|>(\Delta t_{\nu})^{2/5}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}\Big]\leq C\cdot(\Delta t_{\nu})^{1000}

and

\emph{Prob}[|\Delta q^{\sigma}_{\nu}|+|\Delta\zeta_{1,\nu}^{\sigma}|+|\Delta\zeta_{2,\nu}^{\sigma}|>(\Delta t_{\nu})^{2/5}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\leq C\cdot(\Delta t_{\nu})^{1000}.

2
Posterior Probabilities and Expectations

Fix
Q>C
for large enough
C
.

For each
\nu
, let
\text{OK}(\nu)
denote the set of all
(\bar{q}_{1},\dots,\bar{q}_{\nu})\in\mathbb{R}^{\nu}
s.t.

\max_{\mu\leq\nu}|\bar{q}_{\mu}|+|\bar{u}_{\mu}|\leq Q

(2.1)

and

\Big|\sum_{\mu<\nu}(\Delta\bar{q}_{\mu})^{2}-t_{\nu}\Big|\leq Q^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4},

(2.2)

where
\Delta\bar{q}_{\mu}=\bar{q}_{\mu+1}-\bar{q}_{\mu}
,
\bar{q}_{0}\equiv q_{0}
, and
\bar{u}_{\mu}
is defined to be the value assigned to
u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})
provided
q^{\sigma}(t_{\gamma})=\bar{q}_{\gamma}
for
\gamma\leq\mu
.

According to the Bayesian Lemma on Rare Events, we have

\text{Prob}[\text{NOT }\text{OK}(\nu)]\leq\exp(-cQ^{2}).

(2.3)

Thanks to (
1.3
) and Lemma
2
, the following holds for
a\in[-a_{\text{MAX}},a_{\text{MAX}}]
and
(\bar{q}_{1},\dots,\bar{q}_{\nu})\in\text{OK}(\nu)
:

\text{Prob}[a_{\text{TRUE}}\in[a,a+da]\;\text{and}\;q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})\in[\bar{q}_{\mu},\bar{q}_{\mu}+d\bar{q}_{\mu}]\;\text{for}\;\mu\leq\nu]\\
=(1+O(Q^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4}))\cdot(*)\cdot d\bar{q}_{1}\cdots d\bar{q}_{\nu},

where

(*)=\Big[d\text{Prior}(a)\cdot\prod_{0\leq\mu<\nu}\Big\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{2\pi\Delta t_{\mu}}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2\Delta t_{\mu}}(\Delta\bar{q}_{\mu}-(a\bar{q}_{\mu}+\bar{u}_{\mu})\Delta t_{\mu})^{2}\Big)\Big\}\Big].

Note that

(*)=d\text{Prior}(a)\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\zeta}_{2,\nu}a^{2}+\bar{\zeta}_{1,\nu}a\Big)\cdot\{\text{Factor independent of }a\},

where

\bar{\zeta}_{2,\nu}=\bar{\zeta}_{2,\nu}(\bar{q}_{1},\dots,\bar{q}_{\nu})=\sum_{0\leq\mu<\nu}\bar{q}_{\mu}^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}

(2.4)

and

\bar{\zeta}_{1,\nu}=\bar{\zeta}_{1,\nu}(\bar{q}_{1},\dots,\bar{q}_{\nu})=\sum_{0\leq\mu<\nu}\bar{q}_{\mu}(\Delta\bar{q}_{\mu}-\bar{u}_{\mu}\Delta t_{\mu}).

(2.5)

Hence, for

(\bar{q}_{1},\dots,\bar{q}_{\nu})\in\text{OK}(\nu),

the posterior probability distribution for
a_{\text{TRUE}},
given that
q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})=\bar{q}_{\mu}
for
\mu=1,\dots,\nu,
is given by

d\text{Post}(a|\bar{q}_{1},\dots,\bar{q}_{\nu})\\
=(1+O(Q^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4}))\cdot\frac{d\text{Prior}(a)\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\zeta}_{2,\nu}a^{2}+\bar{\zeta}_{1,\nu}a\Big)}{Z}

(2.6)

for a normalizing constant
Z
. Since

\int_{-a_{\text{MAX}}}^{a_{\text{MAX}}}d\text{Post}(a|\bar{q}_{1},\dots,\bar{q}_{\nu})=1,

equation (
2.6
) holds with

Z=\int_{-a_{\text{MAX}}}^{a_{\text{MAX}}}d\text{Prior}(a)\cdot\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\bar{\zeta}_{2,\nu}a^{2}+\bar{\zeta}_{1,\nu}a\Big).

(2.7)

We have thus proven the following result.

Lemma 11

(Lemma on Posterior Probabilities)
.

Suppose
Q\geq C
for a large enough
C
. Fix
\nu
, and suppose
(q^{\sigma}(t_{1}),\dots,q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))
satisfy

\displaystyle\max_{\mu\leq\nu}|q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})|+|u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})|\leq Q,

(2.8)

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{\mu<\nu}(\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}-t_{\nu}\Big|\leq Q^{2}(\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}})^{1/4}.

(2.9)

Then the posterior probability distribution for
a_{\emph{TRUE}}
conditioned on
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}
is given by

\begin{split}d\emph{Post}(a|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma})=&(1+O(Q^{2}(\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}})^{1/4}))\\
&\cdot\frac{\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})a^{2}+\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})a\Big)\cdot d\emph{Prior}(a)}{Z}\end{split}

(2.10)

with

Z=\int_{-a_{\emph{MAX}}}^{a_{\emph{MAX}}}\exp\Big(-\frac{1}{2}\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})b^{2}+\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})b\Big)\ d\emph{Prior}(b).

(2.11)

Corollary 1
.

Under the assumption of the above Lemma, we have

\emph{E}[a_{\emph{TRUE}}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=O(Q^{2}(\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}})^{1/4})+\bar{a}(\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})),

(2.12)

where

\bar{a}(\bar{\zeta_{1}},\bar{\zeta_{2}})\equiv\frac{\int_{-a_{\emph{MAX}}}^{a_{\emph{MAX}}}a\exp\Big(-\frac{a^{2}}{2}\bar{\zeta_{2}}+a\bar{\zeta_{1}}\Big)\ d\emph{Prior}(a)}{\int_{-a_{\emph{MAX}}}^{a_{\emph{MAX}}}\exp\Big(-\frac{a^{2}}{2}\bar{\zeta_{2}}+a\bar{\zeta_{1}}\Big)\ d\emph{Prior}(a)}

(2.13)

for
(\bar{\zeta_{1}},\bar{\zeta_{2}})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Thanks to the above Corollary, formula (
1.7
), and Lemma
4
, we now have the following results.

Lemma 12

(Lemma on Posterior Expectations)
.

Suppose
Q\geq C
for large enough
C
, and assume that
\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}}\leq Q^{-1000}
.

Define the event

\emph{TAME}(\nu)=\{|\Delta q^{\sigma}_{\nu}|\leq 2(\Delta t_{\nu})^{2/5},|\Delta\zeta_{1,\nu}^{\sigma}|\leq 2(\Delta t_{\nu})^{2/5},|\Delta\zeta_{2,\nu}^{\sigma}|\leq 2(\Delta t_{\nu})^{2/5}\}.

Fix
\nu
, and suppose we have

\displaystyle\max_{\mu\leq\nu}|q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})|+|u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})|\leq Q,\;\text{and}

\displaystyle\Big|\sum_{0\leq\mu<\nu}(\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}-t_{\nu}\Big|\leq Q^{2}(\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}})^{1/4}.

Then the following hold.

\displaystyle\emph{E}[(\Delta q^{\sigma}_{\nu})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\emph{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=[\bar{a}(\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))q^{\sigma}_{\nu}+u^{\sigma}_{\nu}](\Delta t_{\nu})+\emph{ERR}\;1,

\displaystyle\emph{E}[(\Delta\zeta_{1,\nu}^{\sigma})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\emph{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=\bar{a}(\zeta_{1,\nu}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2,\nu}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\cdot(q^{\sigma}_{\nu})^{2}(\Delta t_{\nu})+\emph{ERR}\;2,

\displaystyle\emph{E}[(\Delta\zeta_{2,\nu}^{\sigma})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\emph{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=(q^{\sigma}_{\nu})^{2}(\Delta t_{\nu})+\emph{ERR}\;3,

\displaystyle\emph{E}[(\Delta q^{\sigma}_{\nu})^{2}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\emph{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=(\Delta t_{\nu})+\emph{ERR}\;4,

\displaystyle\emph{E}[(\Delta q^{\sigma}_{\nu})\cdot(\Delta\zeta_{1,\nu}^{\sigma})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\emph{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=q^{\sigma}_{\nu}(\Delta t_{\nu})+\emph{ERR}\;5,

\displaystyle\emph{E}[(\Delta q^{\sigma}_{\nu})\cdot(\Delta\zeta_{2,\nu}^{\sigma})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\emph{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=\emph{ERR}\;6,

\displaystyle\emph{E}[(\Delta\zeta_{1,\nu}^{\sigma})^{2}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\emph{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=(q^{\sigma}_{\nu})^{2}(\Delta t_{\nu})+\emph{ERR}\;7,

\displaystyle\emph{E}[(\Delta\zeta_{1,\nu}^{\sigma})(\Delta\zeta_{2,\nu}^{\sigma})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\emph{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}]=\emph{ERR}\;8,

\displaystyle\emph{E}[(\Delta\zeta_{2,\nu}^{\sigma})^{2}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\emph{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=\emph{ERR}\;9,

where

|\emph{ERR}\;1|,\dots,|\emph{ERR}\;9|\leq C^{\prime}Q^{4}(\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}})^{1/4}\Delta t_{\nu}.

Moreover, under the above assumptions on
(q^{\sigma}(t_{1}),\dots,q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))
, we have

\emph{Prob}[\emph{NOTTAME}(\nu)|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\leq(\Delta t_{\nu})^{20}.

Here,

\bar{a}(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2}):=\frac{\int_{-a_{\emph{MAX}}}^{a_{\emph{MAX}}}a\exp\Big(-\frac{a^{2}}{2}\zeta_{2}+a\zeta_{1}\Big)\ d\emph{Prior}(a)}{\int_{-a_{\emph{MAX}}}^{a_{\emph{MAX}}}\exp\Big(-\frac{a^{2}}{2}\zeta_{2}+a\zeta_{1}\Big)\ d\emph{Prior}(a)}.

3
The PDE Assumption

For our given probability distribution
d\text{Prior}(a)
, we fix the function
\bar{a}(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})
given in the preceding section, and we introduce the following PDE for an unknown function
S(q,t,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})
defined on
\mathbb{R}\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}\times[0,\infty)
:

\begin{split}0=&\partial_{t}S+(\bar{a}(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})q+u_{\text{opt}})\partial_{q}S+\bar{a}(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})q^{2}\partial_{\zeta_{1}}S+q^{2}\partial_{\zeta_{2}}S+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{q}^{2}S\\
&+q\partial_{q\zeta_{1}}S+\frac{1}{2}q^{2}\partial_{\zeta_{1}}^{2}S+(q^{2}+u_{\text{opt}}^{2}),\end{split}

(3.1)

where

u_{\text{opt}}=-\frac{1}{2}\partial_{q}S.

(3.2)

We
assume
the existence of a solution of the above PDE, satisfying the following additional conditions.

The
terminal condition
:

S(q,t,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})=0\;\text{at}\;t=T.

(3.3)

Positivity
:

S(q,t,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})\geq 0.

(3.4)

Estimates
: We assume
S\in C^{1,2}
. For
|\alpha|\leq 3
, we have almost everywhere that

|\partial^{\alpha}S(q,t,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})|\leq K\cdot[1+|q|+|\zeta_{1}|+|\zeta_{2}|]^{m_{0}}

(3.5)

for constants
K
,
m_{0}\geq 1
. Moreover,

|u_{\text{opt}}(q,t,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})|\leq C_{\text{TAME}}^{\text{opt}}[|q|+1].

(3.6)

Note that (
3.5
) holds everywhere, not just almost everywhere, when
|\alpha|\leq 2
.

We call
K
,
m_{0}
,
C_{\text{TAME}}^{\text{opt}}
,
a_{\text{MAX}}
, and our upper bounds for
T
and
|q_{0}|
the
boilerplate constants
. We now broaden our definition of constants
c
,
C
,
C^{\prime}
, etc., to allow them to depend on the
boilerplate constants
. As usual, these symbols may denote different constants in different occurrences.

We strengthen our large
Q
assumption. More precisely, we assume from now on that
Q\geq C
for a large enough constant
C
. Since the meaning of the constant
C
has changed, the above is stronger than our previous large
Q
assumption.

We assume that

\Delta t_{\text{MAX}}\leq Q^{-1000}.

4
The Allegedly Optimal Strategy

Let
u_{\text{opt}}=u_{\text{opt}}(q,t,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})
be as in Section
3
.

We define a strategy
\tilde{\sigma}
based on the function
u_{\text{opt}}
.

Given
\nu
(
1\leq\nu\leq N)
and given real numbers
q_{1},\dots,q_{\nu}
, we define numbers
u_{\mu}
,
\zeta_{1,\mu}
,
\zeta_{2,\mu}
by induction on
\mu=0,1,\dots,\nu
so that
\zeta_{1,0}=\zeta_{2,0}=0
, and

\displaystyle u_{\mu}=u_{\text{opt}}(q_{\mu},t_{\mu},\zeta_{1,\mu},\zeta_{2,\mu})

\displaystyle\zeta_{1,\mu}=\sum_{0\leq\gamma<\mu}q_{\gamma}([q_{\gamma+1}-q_{\gamma}]-u_{\gamma}\Delta t_{\gamma})

\displaystyle\zeta_{2,\mu}=\sum_{0\leq\gamma<\mu}q_{\gamma}^{2}\Delta t_{\gamma}

for each
\mu
.

We then set
\tilde{\sigma}_{\nu}(q_{1},\dots,q_{\nu})
equal to the above
u_{\mu}
for
\mu=\nu
.

We define our
allegedly optimal strategy

\tilde{\sigma}
to be the collection of tame rules

\tilde{\sigma}=(\tilde{\sigma}_{\nu})_{\nu=0,1,\dots,N-1}.

Thanks to our PDE Assumption (see (
3.6
)), each
\tilde{\sigma}_{\nu}
is indeed a tame rule with tame constant
C_{\text{TAME}}^{\text{opt}}
, hence
\tilde{\sigma}
is a strategy.

5
Performance of Competing Strategies

We have just defined the allegedly optimal strategy
\tilde{\sigma}
, based on the functions
u_{\text{opt}}(q,t,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})
,
S(q,t,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})
, and
\bar{a}(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})
.

In this section, we compare the performance of
\tilde{\sigma}
with that of an arbitrary competing (deterministic, tame) strategy
\sigma
, also defined with respect to the given partition
0=t_{0}<t_{1}<\dots<t_{N}=T
. We assume that

(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})\leq Q^{-2000m_{0}}.

(5.1)

(See Section
3
for
m_{0}
.) Thanks to our assumption
3.6
, the strategy
\tilde{\sigma}
satisfies

|u^{\tilde{\sigma}}(t_{\nu})|\leq C_{\text{TAME}}^{\text{opt}}[|q^{\tilde{\sigma}}(t_{\nu})|+1],

i.e.,
\tilde{\sigma}
is tame with constant
C_{\text{TAME}}^{\text{opt}}
. We assume that the strategy
\sigma
is tame with constant
C_{\text{TAME}}^{\sigma}
, i.e., we assume that

|u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\leq C_{\text{TAME}}^{\sigma}[|q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|+1].

In this section, we write
c
,
C
,
C^{\prime}
, etc. to denote constants determined by
C_{\text{TAME}}^{\sigma}
and the
boilerplate constants
(one of which is
C_{\text{TAME}}^{\text{opt}}
). We strengthen our large
Q
assumption by supposing that
Q>C
for a large enough
C
. Since the meaning of constants
C
has changed, this indeed strengthens our previous large
Q
assumption.

We define

\mathcal{U}=\{(q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:|q|,|\zeta_{1}|,|\zeta_{2}|\leq Q\}

(5.2)

and

\mathcal{U}^{+}=\{(q,\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})\in\mathbb{R}^{3}:|q|,|\zeta_{1}|,|\zeta_{2}|\leq 2Q\}.

(5.3)

For each
\mu
(
0\leq\mu\leq N
) we introduce the event

\begin{split}\text{OK}_{\mu}=\Big\{&(q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}),\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))\in\mathcal{U},\\
&\Big|\sum_{0\leq\gamma<\mu}(\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\gamma}))^{2}-t_{\mu}\Big|\leq Q^{2}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4}\Big\}.\end{split}

(5.4)

We define the event

\textsc{disaster}=\{\text{OK}_{\mu}\;\text{fails for some }\mu\leq N\}

(5.5)

and the stopping time

\tau=\begin{cases}t_{\mu}\;\text{for the least }\mu\text{ for which }\text{OK}_{\mu}\;\text{fails,}&\text{if there is such a }\mu,\\
T=t_{N}&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

(5.6)

Note that
\tau
is indeed a stopping time with respect to the
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}
, i.e., the event
\tau>t_{\nu}
is
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}
-measurable, for each
\nu
.

We define a
cost-to-go
by setting

\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})=\sum_{t_{\nu}\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\mu}.

(5.7)

To measure the difference between the strategies
\sigma
and
\tilde{\sigma}
, we introduce the random variable

\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})=u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})-u_{\text{opt}}(q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}),t_{\mu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})).

(5.8)

Note that

u_{\text{opt}}(q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}),t_{\mu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))

is not the same as

u^{\tilde{\sigma}}(t_{\mu})=u_{\text{opt}}(q^{\tilde{\sigma}}(t_{\mu}),t_{\mu},\zeta_{1}^{\tilde{\sigma}}(t_{\mu}),\zeta_{2}^{\tilde{\sigma}}(t_{\mu})).

In the next lemma, we compare the cost-to-go of
\sigma
with that of
\tilde{\sigma}
. We will be conditioning on
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}
, so the quantities
q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})
,
u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})
,
\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})
,
\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})
may be regarded as determinisitc.

Lemma 13

(Main Lemma on Competing Strategies)
.

Fix
\nu

(0\leq\nu\leq N)
. Suppose

(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\in\mathcal{U}.

(
\star
)

Then

\begin{split}\emph{E}[\emph{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]+&\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\emph{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
\geq&S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\\
&+\emph{E}\Big[\sum_{t_{\nu}\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}\Big]\\
&-Q^{2m_{0}}(T-t_{\nu})\cdot(\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}})^{1/20}.\end{split}

(A)

If
\sigma=\tilde{\sigma}
, then

\begin{split}\emph{E}[\emph{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\leq&S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\\
&+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\emph{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
&+Q^{2m_{0}}(T-t_{\nu})\cdot(\Delta t_{\emph{MAX}})^{1/20}.\end{split}

(B)

We fix the large constant
\hat{C}
throughout this section.

Proof.

We proceed by downward induction on
\nu
.

In the base case
,
\nu=N
. Since
\tau\leq T=t_{N}
, we have
\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})=0
. Also,
S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))=0
by the terminal condition for our PDE. Moreover, the sum

\sum_{t_{\nu}\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}

is empty, and
T-t_{\nu}=0
. Therefore, (
A
) asserts that

\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\geq 0,

while (
B
) asserts that if
\sigma=\tilde{\sigma}
, then

0\leq\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}].

These two (equivalent) inequalities are obviously correct, so our lemma holds in the base case
\nu=N
.

For the induction step
, we fix
\nu<N
, and assume that (
A
) and (
B
) hold with
(\nu+1)
in place of
\nu
. We will deduce (
A
) and (
B
) for the given
\nu
. To do so, we first dispose of a trivial case.

Suppose for a moment that
\text{OK}_{\mu}
fails for some
\mu\leq\nu
.
Then
disaster
occurs, and
\tau\leq t_{\nu}
; consequently,
\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})=0
, and

\sum_{t_{\nu}\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}=0.

Therefore, (
A
) asserts that

\displaystyle\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\geq S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))-Q^{2m_{0}}(T-t_{\nu})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/2},

while (
B
) asserts that if
\sigma=\tilde{\sigma}
then

0\leq S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}+Q^{2m_{0}}\cdot(T-t_{\nu})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}.

These inequalities are immediate from our assumptions on the PDE solution
S
, together with our hypothesis

(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\in\mathcal{U}.

Thus, our induction step is complete in the trivial case in which
\text{OK}_{\mu}
fails for some
\mu\leq\nu
.

From now on, we assume that

\underline{\text{OK}_{\mu}\;\text{holds for all}\;\mu\leq\nu.}

(5.9)

Thus,

\tau\geq t_{\nu+1}

(5.10)

For the moment, we condition on
\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma},
and distinguish two cases.

\displaystyle\underline{\text{Case I}}:

\displaystyle(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\in\mathcal{U},

\displaystyle\underline{\text{Case II}}:

\displaystyle(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\notin\mathcal{U}

\displaystyle.

In Case I, our inductive hypothesis tells us the following.

\displaystyle\text{E}[\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1})|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]+\hat{C}

\displaystyle Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))

\displaystyle+\text{E}\Big[\sum_{t_{\nu+1}\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}\Big]

\displaystyle-Q^{2m_{0}}(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20};

and if
\sigma=\tilde{\sigma}
then

\displaystyle\text{E}[\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1})|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]\leq

\displaystyle S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))

\displaystyle+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]

\displaystyle+Q^{2m_{0}}(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}.

Since

\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})=\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1})+[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\nu},

thanks to (
5.10
), the above inequalities yield at once that

\begin{split}\text{E}[\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]+\hat{C}&Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]\\
\geq&S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\\
&+[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\nu}\\
&+\text{E}\Big[\sum_{t_{\nu+1}\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}\Big]\\
&-Q^{2m_{0}}\cdot(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20};\end{split}

(AI)

and if
\sigma=\tilde{\sigma}
then

\begin{split}\text{E}[\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]\leq&S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\\
&+[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\nu}\\
&+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]\\
&+Q^{2m_{0}}(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}.\end{split}

(BI)

Estimates (
AI
) and (
BI
) hold in Case I.

We turn to
Case II
. In that case,
\text{OK}_{\nu+1}
fails,
disaster
occurs, and
\tau=t_{\nu+1}
, hence

\displaystyle\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})=[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\nu}

\displaystyle\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]=1

\displaystyle\sum_{t_{\nu+1}\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}=0.

So we have the following estimates:

\begin{split}\text{E}[\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|&\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]\\
\geq&\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}+\text{E}\Big[\sum_{t_{\nu+1}\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}\Big]\\
&-Q^{2m_{0}}(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20};\end{split}

(AII)

and if
\sigma=\tilde{\sigma}
then

\begin{split}\text{E}[\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]\leq&[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\nu}\\
&+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]\\
&+Q^{2m_{0}}\cdot(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}.\end{split}

(BII)

Estimates (
AII
) and (
BII
) hold in Case II.

Combining estimates (
AI
), (
BI
), (
AII
), (
BII
), we obtain the following inequalities, which hold in both Cases I and II:

\displaystyle\text{E}[\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]+\hat{C}

\displaystyle Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{Case}\;\text{I}}

\displaystyle+C\hat{Q}^{2m_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{Case}\;\text{II}}+[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\nu}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{Case}\;\text{I}}

\displaystyle+\text{E}\Big[\sum_{t_{\nu+1}\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}\Big]

\displaystyle-Q^{2m_{0}}\cdot(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20};

and if
\sigma=\tilde{\sigma}
, then

\displaystyle\text{E}[\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]\leq

\displaystyle S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{Case}\;\text{I}}

\displaystyle+[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\nu}

\displaystyle+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}]

\displaystyle+Q^{2m_{0}}\cdot(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}.

We now cease conditioning on
\mathcal{F}_{\nu+1}^{\sigma}
and instead condition on
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}
. From the two preceding inequalities we obtain the following estimates, valid whenever (
5.9
) holds.

\begin{split}\text{E}[\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]&+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
\geq&\text{E}[S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{Case}\;\text{I}}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
&+\text{E}[\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{Case}\;\text{II}}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
&+[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\nu}\cdot\text{E}[\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{Case}\;\text{I}}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
&+\text{E}\Big[\sum_{t_{\nu+1}\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}\Big]\\
&-Q^{2m_{0}}\cdot(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20};\end{split}

(A*)

and if
\sigma=\tilde{\sigma}
then

\begin{split}\text{E}[\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\leq&\text{E}[S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))]\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{Case}\;\text{I}}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
&+[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\nu}\\
&+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
&+Q^{2m_{0}}\cdot(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}.\end{split}

(B*)

Next, from Lemma
12
, recall the event

\text{TAME}(\nu)=\{|\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|,|\Delta\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|,|\Delta\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\leq 2(\Delta t_{\nu})^{2/5}\},

(5.11)

and the estimate

\text{Prob}[\text{NOT TAME}(\nu)|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\leq(\Delta t_{\nu})^{20}.

(5.12)

(Note that Lemma
12
applies, thanks to (
5.9
).)
If
\text{TAME}(\nu)
occurs, then, since

(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\in\mathcal{U}

(by the hypothesis of the present lemma), we have

(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\in\mathcal{U}^{+},

hence

0\leq S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\leq CQ^{m_{0}}.

Therefore, if we take
\hat{C}
large enough, then

S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{Case}\;\text{I}}+\frac{1}{2}\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{Case}\;\text{II}}\\
\geq S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}

(5.13)

and

\frac{1}{2}\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{Case}\;\text{II}}\geq[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\nu}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{Case}\;\text{II}}.

(5.14)

Putting (
5.13
) and (
5.14
) into (
A*
), we learn that

\begin{split}\text{E}[\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]&+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
\geq&\text{E}[S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
&+[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\nu}\text{Prob}[\text{TAME}(\nu)|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
&+\text{E}\Big[\sum_{t_{\nu+1}\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}\Big|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}\Big]\\
&-Q^{2m_{0}}\cdot(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}.\end{split}

(A#)

To obtain an analogous result from (
B*
), we note that

\displaystyle S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),

\displaystyle t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{Case}\;\text{I}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\in\mathcal{U}^{+}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}

\displaystyle+CQ^{2m_{0}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{NOT TAME}(\nu)},

and therefore

\begin{split}\text{E}[S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}&),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{Case}\;\text{I}}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
\leq&\text{E}[S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
&+CQ^{2m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{20},\end{split}

(5.15)

thanks to (
5.12
).

Putting (
5.15
) into (
B*
), we obtain for
\sigma=\tilde{\sigma}
the inequality

\begin{split}\text{E}[\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|&\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
\leq&\text{E}[S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
&+CQ^{2m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{20}+[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\nu}\\
&+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
&+Q^{2m_{0}}(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}.\end{split}

(B#)

Estimates (
A#
) (for general
\sigma
) and (
B#
) (for
\sigma=\tilde{\sigma}
) hold whenever (
5.9
) is satisfied.

We next study the quantity

\text{E}[S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}],

which appears in (
A#
) and (
B#
).

Thanks to (
5.9
), (
5.4
), and (
5.11
), the points
(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))
and
(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))
both lie in
\mathcal{U}^{+}
provided
\text{TAME}(\nu)
holds; see (
5.3
). Recall that the third derivatives of
S
are assumed to be bounded a.e. by
CQ^{m_{0}}
on
\mathcal{U}^{+}
. Therefore, if
\text{TAME}(\nu)
holds, then

\begin{split}\Big|S(q^{\sigma}&(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\\
&-\sum_{p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3}+p_{4}\leq 2}\Big\{\frac{(\partial_{t}^{p_{1}}\partial_{q}^{p_{2}}\partial_{\zeta_{1}}^{p_{3}}\partial_{\zeta_{2}}^{p_{4}}S)}{p_{1}!p_{2}!p_{3}!p_{4}!}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{p_{1}}(\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{p_{2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\cdot(\Delta\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{p_{3}}(\Delta\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{p_{4}}\Big\}\Big|\\
&\leq CQ^{m_{0}}\max\{(\Delta t_{\nu}),|\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|,|\Delta\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|,|\Delta\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\}^{3}\\
&\leq C^{\prime}Q^{m_{0}}[(\Delta t_{\nu})^{2/5}]^{3},\end{split}

(5.16)

where the partials of
S
are evaluated at
(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))
, and we have made use of (
5.11
).

Moreover, the summands in (
5.16
) satisfy

|(\partial_{t}^{p_{1}}\cdots\partial_{\zeta_{2}}^{p_{4}}S)(\Delta t_{\nu})^{p_{1}}(\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{p_{2}}(\Delta\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{p_{3}}(\Delta\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{p_{4}}|\\
\leq CQ^{m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{p_{1}+\frac{2}{5}[p_{2}+p_{3}+p_{4}]}

whenever (
5.9
) and
\text{TAME}(\nu)
hold.

Consequently, (
5.16
) implies that

\begin{split}\Big|S(q^{\sigma}&(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}\\
&-\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}p_{1}+p_{2}+p_{3}+p_{4}\leq 2\\
p_{1}+\frac{2}{5}(p_{2}+p_{3}+p_{4})\leq 1\end{subarray}}\Big\{\frac{(\partial_{t}^{p_{1}}\partial_{q}^{p_{2}}\partial_{\zeta_{1}}^{p_{3}}\partial_{\zeta_{2}}^{p_{4}}S)}{p_{1}!p_{2}!p_{3}!p_{4}!}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{p_{1}}(\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{p_{2}}\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\cdot(\Delta\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{p_{3}}(\Delta\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{p_{4}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}\Big\}\Big|\\
&\leq CQ^{m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{6/5}.\end{split}

(5.17)

The summands entering into (
5.17
) arise from
S
,
\partial_{t}S
,
\partial_{q}S
,
\partial_{\zeta_{1}}S
,
\partial_{\zeta_{2}}S
,
\partial_{q}^{2}S
,
\partial_{q\zeta_{1}}S
,
\partial_{q\zeta_{2}}S
,
\partial_{\zeta_{1}}^{2}S
,
\partial_{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}S
,
\partial_{\zeta_{2}}^{2}S
. We conclude that

\begin{split}|S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}-&\sum_{i=0}^{10}\textsc{Term}\;i|\\
&\leq CQ^{m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{6/5}\end{split}

(5.18)

whenever (
5.9
) holds, where

\displaystyle\textsc{Term}\;0=S\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)},

(5.19)

\displaystyle\textsc{Term}\;1=(\partial_{t}S)\cdot(\Delta t_{\nu})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)},

(5.20)

\displaystyle\textsc{Term}\;2=(\partial_{q}S)\cdot(\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)},

(5.21)

\displaystyle\textsc{Term}\;3=(\partial_{\zeta_{1}}S)\cdot(\Delta\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)},

(5.22)

\displaystyle\textsc{Term}\;4=(\partial_{\zeta_{2}}S)\cdot(\Delta\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)},

(5.23)

\displaystyle\textsc{Term}\;5=\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{q}^{2}S)\cdot(\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)},

(5.24)

\displaystyle\textsc{Term}\;6=(\partial_{q\zeta_{1}}S)\cdot(\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))(\Delta\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)},

(5.25)

\displaystyle\textsc{Term}\;7=(\partial_{q\zeta_{2}}S)\cdot(\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))(\Delta\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)},

(5.26)

\displaystyle\textsc{Term}\;8=\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{\zeta_{1}}^{2}S)\cdot(\Delta\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)},

(5.27)

\displaystyle\textsc{Term}\;9=(\partial_{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}S)\cdot(\Delta\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))(\Delta\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)},

(5.28)

\displaystyle\textsc{Term}\;10=\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{\zeta_{2}}^{2}S)\cdot(\Delta\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}.

(5.29)

Here, again,
S
and its partial derivatives are evaluated at

(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}));

hence these
(\partial^{\alpha}S)
are deterministic once we condition on
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}
. Consequently,

\displaystyle\text{E}[\textsc{Term}\;0|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=S\cdot\text{Prob}[\text{TAME}(\nu)|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}],

\displaystyle\text{E}[\textsc{Term}\;1|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=(\partial_{t}S)\cdot(\Delta t_{\nu})\cdot\text{Prob}[\text{TAME}(\nu)|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}],

\displaystyle\text{E}[\textsc{Term}\;2|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=(\partial_{q}S)\cdot\text{E}[\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}],

\displaystyle\text{E}[\textsc{Term}\;3|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=(\partial_{\zeta_{1}}S)\cdot\text{E}[\Delta\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}],

\displaystyle\text{E}[\textsc{Term}\;4|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=(\partial_{\zeta_{2}}S)\cdot\text{E}[\Delta\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}],

\displaystyle\text{E}[\textsc{Term}\;5|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{q}^{2}S)\cdot\text{E}[(\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}],

\displaystyle\text{E}[\textsc{Term}\;6|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=(\partial_{q\zeta_{1}}S)\cdot\text{E}[(\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))(\Delta\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}],

\displaystyle\text{E}[\textsc{Term}\;7|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=(\partial_{q\zeta_{2}}S)\cdot\text{E}[(\Delta q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))(\Delta\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}],

\displaystyle\text{E}[\textsc{Term}\;8|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{\zeta_{1}}^{2}S)\cdot\text{E}[(\Delta\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}],

\displaystyle\text{E}[\textsc{Term}\;9|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=(\partial_{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}}S)\cdot\text{E}[(\Delta\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))(\Delta\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}],

\displaystyle\text{E}[\textsc{Term}\;10|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]=\frac{1}{2}(\partial_{\zeta_{2}}^{2}S)\cdot\text{E}[(\Delta\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}].

The expectations on the right-hand sides have been computed modulo a small error in Lemma
12
. Applying that lemma, recalling that
|\partial^{\alpha}S|\leq CQ^{m_{0}}
for
|\alpha|\leq 2
, and substituting the results into (
5.18
), we find that

\begin{split}\text{E}[S(q^{\sigma}&(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
&=S+(\Delta t_{\nu})\{\partial_{t}S+[\bar{a}q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})+u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})]\partial_{q}S+\bar{a}\cdot(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}\partial_{\zeta_{1}}S\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}\partial_{\zeta_{2}}S+\frac{1}{2}\partial_{q}^{2}S+q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})\partial_{q\zeta_{1}}S\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{2}(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}\partial_{\zeta_{1}}^{2}S\}+\text{ERROR},\end{split}

(5.30)

where
S
and its derivatives are evaluated at
(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})),

\bar{a}
denotes
\bar{a}(\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})),
and

\begin{split}|\text{ERROR}|&\leq CQ^{m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{6/5}+CQ^{m_{0}+4}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/4}\Delta t_{\nu}\\
&\leq CQ^{m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/5}\Delta t_{\nu}.\end{split}

(5.31)

(We have used our assumption (
5.1
).) Since
S
satisfies our PDE (
3.1
), equation (
5.30
) may be rewritten in the equivalent form

\begin{split}\text{E}[S(q^{\sigma}&(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
=&S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\\
&+(\Delta t_{\nu})\cdot\{(\partial_{q}S)\cdot[u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})-u_{\text{opt}}(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))]\\
&\qquad\qquad\quad-[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u_{\text{opt}}(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})))^{2}]\}\\
&+\text{ERROR},\end{split}

(5.32)

with

|\text{ERROR}|\leq CQ^{m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/5}\Delta t_{\nu}.

(5.33)

Recalling that
\partial_{q}S=-2u_{\text{opt}}
(see (
3.2
)), we see that the expression in curly brackets in (
5.32
) is equal to

\{-2u_{\text{opt}}\cdot[u^{\sigma}-u_{\text{opt}}]-(q^{\sigma})^{2}-u_{\text{opt}}^{2}\}=\{(u^{\sigma}-u_{\text{opt}})^{2}-(q^{\sigma})^{2}-(u^{\sigma})^{2}\},

where we have written
u^{\sigma}
for
u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})
, and
u_{\text{opt}}
for
u_{\text{opt}}(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))
. Moreover,

\displaystyle u^{\sigma}-u_{\text{opt}}

\displaystyle=u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})-u_{\text{opt}}(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))

\displaystyle\equiv\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})

(see (
5.8
)). Therefore, (
5.32
), (
5.33
) are equivalent to:

\begin{split}\text{E}[S(q^{\sigma}&(t_{\nu+1}),t_{\nu+1},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu+1}))\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\text{TAME}(\nu)}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
=&S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\\
&+(\Delta t_{\nu})\{(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}-(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}-(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}\}\\
&+\text{ERROR},\end{split}

(5.34)

with

|\text{ERROR}|\leq CQ^{m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/5}\Delta t_{\nu}.

(5.35)

Here, (
5.34
) and (
5.35
) are valid wherever (
5.9
) holds.

We now substitute (
5.34
), (
5.35
) into (
A#
) and (
B#
), to obtain the following results, valid whenever (
5.9
) holds.

\begin{split}\text{E}[\text{CTG}^{\sigma}&(t_{\nu})|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
\geq&\{S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))+(\Delta t_{\nu})(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}\\
&-[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\nu}-CQ^{m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/5}\Delta t_{\nu}\}\\
&+[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\nu}\cdot\text{Prob}[\text{TAME}(\nu)|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
&+\text{E}\Big[\sum_{t_{\nu+1}\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}\Big]\\
&-Q^{2m_{0}}(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20};\end{split}

(A##)

and if
\sigma=\tilde{\sigma}
, then (since
\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})=0)
we have

\begin{split}\text{E}[\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\leq&\{S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))\\
&+CQ^{m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/5}\Delta t_{\nu}\}+CQ^{2m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{20}\\
&+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\\
&+Q^{2m_{0}}(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}.\end{split}

(B##)

In (
A##
) we note that

[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\text{Prob}[\text{NOT TAME}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\leq CQ^{2}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{20};

see hypothesis (

⋆

‣
13
) of this lemma. Therefore, in (
A##
), the terms

[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\nu}\cdot\text{Prob}[\text{TAME}(\nu)]

and

-[(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}]\Delta t_{\nu}

nearly cancel—they produce a term dominated by
CQ^{2}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{20}
. Moreover, thanks to (
5.10
), we have

(\Delta t_{\nu})(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))^{2}+\text{E}\Big[\sum_{t_{\nu+1}\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}\Big|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}\Big]\\
=\text{E}\Big[\sum_{t_{\nu}\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}\Big|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}\Big].

(We have also used the fact that
\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})
is deterministic when conditioned on
\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}
.)

Finally, our assumptions that
\Delta t_{\text{MAX}}\leq Q^{-2000m_{0}}
and
Q>C
(
C
large enough) imply that

\displaystyle CQ^{2}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{20}

\displaystyle+CQ^{m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/5}\Delta t_{\nu}+Q^{2m_{0}}(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}

\displaystyle\leq Q^{2m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}\cdot(t_{\nu+1}-t_{\nu})+Q^{2m_{0}}(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}

\displaystyle=Q^{2m_{0}}(T-t_{\nu})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}

and also that the sum of terms

CQ^{m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/5}(\Delta t_{\nu})+CQ^{2m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\nu})^{20}+Q^{2m_{0}}(T-t_{\nu+1})(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}

is at most

Q^{2m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}\Delta t_{\nu}+Q^{2m_{0}}(T-t_{\nu+1})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}\\
=Q^{2m_{0}}(T-t_{\nu})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}.

In view of the above remarks, (
A##
) and (
B##
) imply the following results, valid whenever (
5.9
) holds.

\displaystyle\text{E}[\text{CTG}^{\sigma}

\displaystyle(t_{\nu})|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))+\text{E}\Big[\sum_{t_{\nu}\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}\Big|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}\Big]

\displaystyle-Q^{2m_{0}}\cdot(T-t_{\nu})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20};

and if
\sigma=\tilde{\sigma}
, then

\displaystyle\text{E}[\text{CTG}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu})|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]\leq

\displaystyle S(q^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),t_{\nu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\nu}))+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}|\mathcal{F}_{\nu}^{\sigma}]

\displaystyle+Q^{2m_{0}}\cdot(T-t_{\nu})\cdot(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}.

These are precisely our desired conclusions (
A
) and (
B
). Our downward induction on
\nu
is complete, thus proving Lemma
13
.
∎

We now draw conclusions from Lemma
13
. Setting
\nu=0
, we obtain the following results, comparing the allegedly optimal strategy
\tilde{\sigma}
to the competing strategy
\sigma
.

\begin{split}\text{E}\Big[\sum_{0\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}&\{(q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\}\Delta t_{\mu}\Big]+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}]\\
\geq&S(q_{0},0,0,0)+\text{E}\Big[\sum_{0\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}\Big]\\
&-Q^{2m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}T;\end{split}

(5.36)

and if
\sigma=\tilde{\sigma}
, then

\begin{split}\text{E}\Big[\sum_{0\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}\{(q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}&(t_{\mu}))^{2}\}\Delta t_{\mu}\Big]\\
\leq&S(q_{0},0,0,0)+\hat{C}Q^{2m_{0}}\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}]\\
&+Q^{2m_{0}}(\Delta t_{\text{MAX}})^{1/20}T.\end{split}

(5.37)

From (
5.2
), (
5.4
), (
5.5
) and Lemma
10
, we have

\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}]\leq C\exp(-cQ).

(5.38)

Let us investigate what happens if
disaster
occurs.

Since

|u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})|\leq C[|q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})|+1],

we have

\sum_{0\leq\mu<N}\{(q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\}\Delta t_{\mu}\leq C(\max_{\mu}|q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})|)^{2}+C.

Also,

\displaystyle|\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})|

\displaystyle=|u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})-u_{\text{opt}}(q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}),t_{\mu},\zeta_{1}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}),\zeta_{2}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))|

\displaystyle\leq|u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})|+C[|q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})|+1]

by our PDE Assumption (see (
3.6
)). Hence,

\sum_{0\leq\mu<N}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}\leq C(\max_{\mu}|q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu})|)^{2}+C.

The above remarks and Lemma
10
yield the estimates:

\text{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{0\leq\mu<N}\{(q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\}\Delta t_{\mu}\Big)^{2}\Big]\leq C

and

\text{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{0\leq\mu<N}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}\Big)^{2}\Big]\leq C.

Consequently, Cauchy-Schwarz and (
5.38
) imply that

\begin{split}\text{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{0\leq\mu<N}\{(q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\}\Delta t_{\mu}\Big)\cdot&\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{disaster}}\Big]\\
&\leq C\cdot(\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}])^{1/2}\\
&\leq C^{\prime}\exp(-c^{\prime}Q)\end{split}

(5.39)

and

\begin{split}\text{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{0\leq\mu<N}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}\Big)\cdot&\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{disaster}}\Big]\\
&\leq C\cdot(\text{Prob}[\textsc{disaster}])^{1/2}\\
&\leq C^{\prime}\exp(-cQ).\end{split}

(5.40)

So we have controlled the consequences of
disaster
.

On the other hand, if
disaster
does not occur, then
\tau=T
; see (
5.5
) and (
5.6
).

Therefore,

\begin{split}\text{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{0\leq\mu<N}\{(q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}+&(u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\}\Delta t_{\mu}\Big)\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{non-disaster}}\Big]\\
&\leq\text{E}\Big[\sum_{0\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}\{(q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\}\Delta t_{\mu}\Big]\end{split}

(5.41)

and

\begin{split}\text{E}\Big[\Big(\sum_{0\leq\mu<N}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}\Big)&\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\textsc{non-disaster}}\Big]\\
&\leq\text{E}\Big[\sum_{0\leq t_{\mu}<\tau}(\textsc{Discrep}^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}\Delta t_{\mu}\Big].\end{split}

(5.42)

Also, obviously,

\begin{split}\text{E}\Big[\sum_{0\leq\mu<N}\{(q^{\sigma}(t_{\mu}))^{2}+(u^{\sigma}&(t_{\mu}))^{2}\}\Delta t_{\mu}\Big]\\
